# Caliper Harness — Construction Plan

Created: 2026-10-06. Evidence basis: Concept.md (same directory). Status: living file — update in place.
Rule: on any conflict between this plan and Concept.md, Concept.md wins.

Current product principles: [guidingprinciples.md](guidingprinciples.md). Read this short owner-direction reference with the operating rules before implementation or qualification.

Current test reporting contract: [CapabilityRubric.md](CapabilityRubric.md). Every test run/live experiment reports the same eighteen MECE function domains, scope/candidate/model/evidence, visible unexercised rows, outcome, change and primary cause/next action. Component success is separate from native coding; the rubric is discussed with the owner after each run. This adds reporting discipline, not another test framework or qualification score.

## How to use this file

Read top-down: rules (§0), recipes, then phases in order. Phases run sequentially unless marked parallel-safe. Task IDs are stable addresses — never renumber. Every tick carries a LOG line with evidence. No tick without a LOG is verified.

## Priority ladder

- P0: usable, unambiguous model instructions and running foundations (scaffold, config, model round-trip).
- P1: core value (agent loop, state, planning, subagents).
- P2: quality and autonomy (decision model, context management, TUI).
- P3: evolution and experiments (feedback loop, error wiki, 5k challenge).

## §0 Operating rules (bind every session and subagent)

1. No stubs, placeholders, or TODO-later. Incomplete → stop and escalate.
2. No fake success in code or process. No simulation fallbacks, no silent downgrades, no tick without evidence.
3. Loud, actionable failures only. Missing dependency or unreachable model → hard error naming the fix.
4. Tests must fail on pre-fix code. State how you checked.
5. All tunables in YAML. No hardcoded thresholds, splits, or behavior switches.
6. Every interface must pass the small-model test: usable correctly by a weak executor.
7. Local only. No pushes, no paid triggers without explicit approval.
8. Definition of Done: code + tests + docs updated + live verification + tick with LOG line.
9. Lightweight code only: fast, simple, easy to follow. No massive type checks, no overhedging. Every fix addresses the root cause and leaves the codebase clean and versatile. A completely unfamiliar programmer should be able to completely understand the codebase in a single afternoon.

## Verification recipe catalog

- V-BUILD: `go build ./... && go vet ./...` green from repo root.
- V-TEST: `go test ./...` green; new tests fail on pre-fix code (stash-check).
- V-LIVE: run the real binary against a local model; exercise the claimed behavior end to end.
- V-TUI: drive the TUI with scripted keys; assert views render and respond.

## Phase 2: State layer [P1]

Objective: embedded GraphQL over chosen storage; memory read/write/recall; JSONL sessions with retention.

Build order: T2.2 → T2.1 → T2.3 → T2.4 → T2.5 (IDs stay stable per plan rule — T2.1 lands on the real T2.2 store, no throwaway scaffold). - [x] **T2.4 — JSONL sessions + retention.**
  - TARGET: every run appends timestamped events to sessions/<id>.jsonl; retention sweeper deletes sessions untouched for N months (default 3, YAML); GraphQL sessions/session(id) index them.
  - SKETCH: internal/session (writer, reader, sweep) owns ALL session write logic — the one file edits concentrate on; mutation resolvers are thin wrappers over it (a resolver containing session logic fails review). Index by scanning dir (no separate DB until scale demands it).
  - FALLBACK (founder-sanctioned): if the T2.5 live run shows per-event mutation overhead slowing the loop, or a resolver grows past a thin wrapper, the loop flips its call site to direct session-package calls — the same functions the resolvers call, zero duplication, no rewrite.

## Phase 3: Planning and subagents [P1]

Objective: Plan/Approach/Task lifecycle in code; templates + validation; one-call approach launch; workflows; retire commands.
Planning inputs (founder, 2026-10-06): workflows and strict validation are required; §0.9 lightweight rule binds the design. Task list drafted in planning session before any build.
Founder rulings 2026-10-06: (1) deterministic validation only, gate-hook seams for Phase 4 judgment; (2) sequential AND concurrent fan-out, YAML-chosen, per-subagent context window YAML, autodetected max-subagents (user-overridable); (3) minimal workflows (sequential queue + retries + collection); (4) template files, as short as possible (must fit authoring context). Assumptions ratified (no objection): PAT migration now; retire = state flip + CLI.

Fix 1 (fp32 mask) did NOT stop the crash — identical frames. Fix 2: backend retains recent feeds (E5 use-after-free: async reset deallocs numpy already GC'd; _PyObject_Free in the faulting frames). Parity re-verified. Crash fixed (100+ judgments, clean shutdowns, no new .ips). Steady-state npu 0.39s vs torch-cpu 0.80s. BUT: powermetrics over 20 npu judgments shows ANE Power 0mW on all 8 samples — the ANE never engages; Core ML runs the graph on GPU/CPU (E5 streams exist, silicon idle). Likely cause: mask/gather/index ops aren't ANE-placeable, silent fallback. Founder decision: solve ANE now, reliably + autonomously. Found laya-apple (3rd-party, Apache-2.0, PyPI 1.6.3): solved Laya-on-ANE via split architecture (host codec + ANE transformer blocks, BC1S, additive masks) + compute-plan gate + runtime timing probe. Built placement.py (compute-plan X-ray, same technique) — sandbox-poisons it (ANE compile denied), so the real reading needs one founder run. Placement X-ray (founder run): 640 ANE / 596 GPU / 1 CPU, 62 transitions. ANE takes linears/norms/gelus; GPU keeps matmul(60)+softmax(31) (attention core), gather*(4) (embedding+markers), topk/log/reduce/equal/and (head). Runtime evidently refuses the 62-transition ping-pong and runs all-GPU (ANE 0mW). Matches laya-apple's architecture exactly (host: embedding/masks/markers/head; ANE: transformer blocks). Proposed: adopt laya-apple (Apache-2.0, pinned) as the ANE runtime behind our unchanged /judge API. Founder confirmed adoption (only the decision model runs on ANE; main LLMs stay on GPU). Facts verified (README+guide: device ane|gpu|auto, buckets 64/96/128, build+calibrate flow, error taxonomy, answer_confidence is the gating quantity — already our contract). Implemented: server.py NPU path now delegates to laya-apple (npu→device ane, auto→device auto, cpu/gpu stay torch; ane→npu label map; 6 artifact/backend errors→501, UnsupportedShape→400; --model restricted to the 3 shorts, fail-fast; guarded mlx.core preload first — mlx-after-torch can SIGABRT per ml-stack §1b). Deleted backend_coreml.py/convert.py/placement.py (sweep: only this LOG mentions them). requirements-npu.txt pins laya-apple[ane]==1.6.3 (PyPI-confirmed) with the one-shot torch-2.7 build jail. Calibrate+predict live: explicit ANE works (pick agrees, 0.77 vs torch 0.7551, Δ0.015 within the 0.02 gate, L64 bucket, 20.9ms). Calibration surprise: on the M3 Ultra MLX beats ANE at every bucket (L64 8.37 vs 10.97ms), so laya-apple's latency auto would never use the ANE here — our auto is ANE-first instead (spill to MLX only on shape; rationale: solo latency isn't the goal, keeping decisions off the generation GPU is). Discriminating run: their own tight benchmark under ane+gpu power. If ANE moves, case closed; if GPU moves, E5 violates compute units (upstream escalation); Power A/B #3: tight benchmark (60 forwards, 11–14ms, L256 cleanly refused) under 40 samples — ANE 0 on all 40 (96 total across runs), GPU alive 58–363mW. Latency corroboration: sidecar npu 0.063s vs cpu 1.884s (30×). So: plan 100%/0trans + probe passed + 30× CPU + correct verdicts, but the rail reads dead. Hypotheses ranked: (C) M3 Ultra ane_power sampler broken — 96 consecutive exact-zeros on a busy desktop with aned+photoanalysisd active; (A) E5 on GPU despite CPU_AND_NE; (B) sub-floor efficiency. Discriminator: idle baseline vs sustained-ANE vs MLX-GPU-positive-control power runs. ## Phase 5: Context management [P2]

Objective: server-measured usage accounting; 90/40/10+1% compaction with chained worker-written hardened summaries; deterministic in-flight pruning (20% outputs, 5%/3-latest schemas); file-backed behavior injection; deterministic memory recall + recall tool.
Invariants: the session record keeps everything verbatim — pruning/compaction touch only the model-bound transcript. Per-step order: stub-prune → measure → compact at ≥90%. No estimator on the governing path; absent server usage → loud warn + no compaction.

## Phase 6: TUI [P2]

Objective: Bubble Tea app in the same binary (`tui` command, in-process over internal/): chat, workview, plan tree, status, sessions, decisions, workflows, files/diff, settings. Slash-first guidance, keyboard secondary nav, Specific Labs terminal translation (green #4AF6C3 accent, mono scale, thin separators, one-line rows, uppercase micro-labels).
Locked (founder talk 2026-10-07): watcher = fsnotify on working tree recursive, debounced, content-hash diffs (config paths later); feedback queue shared by workview + plan tree, rides with next chat message as pending chips (plan feedback next-run only, no node write); recall auto-primary (auto-insert + model self-invocation exist; decision-guided later), /recall manual stays; /status replaces /decision (separate screen: endpoint health/latency/loaded, harness state; use/start/load/unload, decision device, runtime YAML write-back); load/unload Ollama-flavored, MLX restart-with-model; start recipes (macOS built-ins: ollama, mlx_lm.server, decision sidecar; data-driven table, other MLX-based servers via config); supervision v1 = start/stop/status + log tail; live views polling-first (workview watcher excepted); new deps bubbletea/bubbles/lipgloss/fsnotify only.
Slash registry (22): /chat /diff /plans /sessions /decisions /workflows /files /settings /status /models /new /stop /retry /auth /recall /remember /compact /context /clear /feedback /help /quit. Views accept optional targets (/sessions <id>). Registry pattern: each view contributes commands + completion context.

#### Proposed repair plan for owner discussion

**Discussion status:** the owner subsequently authorized the repair plan; the implementation authorization and D01–D14 below supersede this section's original pre-discussion stop. Continue the accepted repairs and their evidence gates. Final objective/coverage audit, README/guide readiness and Phase7/8 remain pending. This is a repair of the existing small harness, not a new platform. Keep the Go executable, in-process Bubble Tea, Kuzu, shared Runner, owning plan package, thin GraphQL bindings and existing typed decision HTTP boundary. Preserve strict YAML typo protection and the separately recorded design choices unless the owner changes them.

Use seven work packages rather than treating39 findings as39 independent projects. The detailed R contracts below remain authoritative; this table gives their sequence, dependencies and proof gates. Some small fixes can proceed in parallel after the discussion, but shared-state ownership precedes workflow qualification and effective request controls precede any new coding comparison.

| Order | Work package / findings | Smallest intended change | Acceptance gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Agree the current core contract — R27, with the policy choices in R02/R03/R10/R30/R31 | Record decisions on completed/stopped/unverified states and CLI exits, required executed checks, state API/fields/mandatory gates, context measurement/response retrieval, supported install path and decision-runtime compatibility. Split accepted missing mechanisms into bounded tasks; do not turn R27 into a large speculative rewrite. | Each concept/implementation discrepancy has an explicit implemented target, owner-approved amendment, or named deferral with its user consequence. Historical ticks/logs are preserved. No silent weakening of capability claims. |
| B | Trustworthy work and state — R01–R07, R34, R36 | One database owner/writer per canonical path; atomic legal transitions; typed outcomes and accurate review/count/exit reporting; context through owned work/cancellation; complete public workflow transitions; refuse incomplete streams; validated native calls executed serially, one text tool block, and verbatim multiline values. Use actual tool/check evidence before verified promotion. | All acknowledged writes persist across handles/close/reopen. Stop settles owned work with no later marker. Multiple tool blocks have no partial side effects. Model-only workflow construction reaches ready/launch legally. Empty/broken files, failed/absent checks, reviewer errors, transport truncation and managed stops cannot qualify verified success. |
| C | Bounded, durable context — R08, R10–R12, R14, R26, R28 | Unicode-safe recall; one returned Runner continuation per chat; origin/revision-bound asynchronous results; message provenance and protected latest-three schemas; bounded behavior shared by executing roles; honest occupancy/spend and next-request admission; useful summaries and bounded I/O. Reuse existing transcript/session/event paths. | Real original intent/latest-three schemas survive combined pruning defaults. Summary→solver→new user→resume preserves accepted notes and relevant failure facts without resurrecting discarded history. Parent/worker/reviewer carry applicable capped behavior. Fixed harness-owned context stays<10k and preferably<5k; unknown/estimated counts are labeled. Actual output/time/capture bounds hold. |
| D | Reliable local runtimes and open decision selection — R09, R15–R18, R20, R31, R35 | Finite settings; shared URL/health/parameter translation; actual installed/selected/loading/active identity; route Models into existing Status instead of a duplicate screen; bind load/unload to selected owned server. Add thin compatible decision loaders behind the existing contract with open checkpoint/path selection and genuine confidence. | Settings affect the next real request or disclose unsupported/pending state. Own Ollama/MLX plain/SSE, device/model changes and failure recovery preserve external workloads. Two real decision families plus another compatible checkpoint activate by configuration without source edits; incompatible interfaces fail clearly, without invented confidence or fallback. |
| E | Usable, faithful views and tools — R13, R19, R21–R25, R32/R33, R38/R39 | Fix derived labels, current Ask text, search path/glob/global-limit semantics, file refresh/wrapping/indent/line mapping, honest first-diff baseline, stable references and agreed last-use retention; preserve fetched pre bytes. Reuse current views/parsers/watchers. | Help/Status remain navigable at80x24; mixed Ask answers/cancel are accurate; source→view→quote and fetched code retain meaningful bytes; actual diff/ref/retention behavior matches labels; file and workflow navigation work on populated state. |
| F | Discoverable installation and learning — R30, R37 | One standard-library CLI/help convention and one recommended first-use path; relocatable native library/assets/runtime layout. Prefer supplying/embedding tiny templates over cwd-sensitive lookup. Keep GB models in explicit user caches. Write the concise guide only after behavior is corrected. | Help has no effects; invalid arguments fail before work. Clean supported Mac installation works outside the source tree/Go cache. A newcomer chooses a compatible local model, makes one independently correct code change and recovers from one error using only shipped instructions. Installation/download and warm-start timing are separate. |
| G | Requalify and reconcile claims — R29 | Red-before-fix assertions on preserved source, focused meaningful regressions, full required gates, then real local-model journeys. Correct inventory/completion statements against receipts; do not replace the negative evaluation with a rescued demo. | All accepted repair gates pass; required live views/workflows have actual receipts. Original coding pilot is rerun against its original task snapshot with shared corrected tools/effective decoding in both arms. Fresh unseen work tests capability claims; no superiority claim from the current0/4 versus0/4 pilot. Only then consider Phase6 exit and drafting Phase7 tasks. |

Dependencies: B's store ownership is foundational for B's graph/workflow and C's persistence. R36 and D's effective generation controls must be in place before interpreting fresh model coding quality. C's origin binding precedes adopting authoritative returned history. D's model activation/identity and B's truthful work precede F's install-and-first-task gate. E/F's help and small parser/view fixes can be parallel work without expanding the architecture. G is the final acceptance pass, not a substitute for B–F.

The owner discussion should settle these choices explicitly:

- **Success and review:** recommend completed execution plus separately visible verification state; required failed/missing checks cannot become verified success. Keep partial work on review errors and managed stops. Choose persisted state names, CLI exit policy and explicit resume/retry semantics; do not blindly retry stopped loops.
- **Concept/state boundary:** choose whether to retain exclusive GraphQL access and implement its missing fields/gates, or amend the current contract to direct owning-package operations plus public GraphQL. Recommend one invariant owner and one legal transition implementation either way; no second state engine. Decide planning articles/rules/current context, response/thinking retrieval, model-written memories and decision compaction triage separately.
- **Context measurement:** choose the exact supported preflight counter boundary versus clearly labeled estimates/unknowns with bounded backend generation. A previous server reading or byte ratio is not an exact measurement of the next prompt. Preserve load-bearing context and provide original event references; do not solve this with a large prompt or an unqualified generic tokenizer layer.
- **Model choice and ownership:** open decision checkpoint selection within supported judgment/runtime interfaces, with actual identity/device/readiness and explicit incompatibility. Unknown confidence must not be guessed. Owned services can apply changes; external services require reconnect/pending instructions and stay under their owner's control.
- **Public readiness:** decide whether the first supported install is a relocatable Mac bundle or a single-file extraction layout, which local backend is the recommended first path, and clean-host/newcomer test access. R30 was historically deferred; it now directly blocks the requested install-and-run claim, even though that historical deferral does not itself rewrite Phase6's old gate.

**Simplicity acceptance:** record handwritten source/file/dependency changes, consolidate duplicate argument/URL/history/rendering paths, and justify each new abstraction. No generic plugin platform, second database/API, giant success-claim parser, batch language or new evaluation framework. Produce a short reading map and have a programmer trace chat→tool→auth→execute→persist→render, launch→review→state, and compact→next turn in an afternoon. The frozen paired-episode source measurement is 26,973 handwritten Go lines/90 files, excluding generated Graph bindings and1,500 vendored Go lines;271 test files contain42,287 lines. The older13,991/59 snapshot is historical. These measurements exceed the lightweight target and do not qualify afternoon comprehension. Tests/generated bindings are reported separately, not hidden. Keep simplification and a short reading-map/real comprehension review as an open release gate; avoid more framework growth.

**Capability acceptance:** fixed prompts already measure roughly1.6–1.7k tokens with all16 schemas, so instruction bloat is unjustified. Rerunning the tiny original pilot is a regression gate, not a significant-uplift claim. Before advertising improved small-model coding, agree a prospective matched task set across real codebases/model families, independent accepted outputs, fixed budgets and a correctness target; preserve every failure/intervention/cost. Normal use must succeed without operator graph seeding or tool-format rescue.

**Schedule, provisional (owner correction):** the initial2–3-week estimate below the proposed repair plan is withdrawn. Owner expects a couple of focused days; current working target is1–2 focused days for the remaining remediation and construction gates, conditional on genuine ordinary small-model coding, packaging and beginner acceptance. Completed component controls do not establish readiness. Re-estimate from current actual blockers; do not expand the project into new frameworks or a repeated prompt sweep.

**Authorization:** the owner subsequently committed to the mapped fixes and settled D01–D16. Implement within those contracts, preserve negative results, and qualify each R task against its full acceptance. Owner-reserved defaults or new contract decisions still need an answer. Phase6exit and Phase7/8 wait for the recorded remediation gate; this original proposal is not a present implementation hold.
